Source simulation outliers: assembly, measurements, and fixes

Publication note: This snapshot includes the measurements, profiling summaries, scheduling evidence, and validation records below. Raw disassembly, complete color plans, and individual sampling files remain in the local audit directory livehd/repros/sim_verilator_outliers_20261013/; they are not part of this published HTML.

Scope: all 46 designs with at least one source LLVM frontend at least 2× slower than Verilator in the supplied report (67 frontend cases). Netlist and formal work are excluded. Both source frontends are retained for every design.

The starting LLVM binaries are from run 20261013T120732. All 46 Verilator cases were freshly rebuilt and run in 20261013T132214. Baseline paired measurements run the binaries serially, alternate their order, retain three samples, and compare checksums at identical cycle counts. The short measurements use 10% of configured cycles, at least 100,000. Full runs additionally check the immutable recorded golden checksum. No checksum is re-recorded.

Findings and retained changes

  • Bit-level boundary expansion: imported fixed bit slices used one 64-bit ABI carrier per bit. The CDC FIFO had 2,335 slots, including 2,238 one-bit slots; its largest adapter exceeded 34,000 instructions. Shared machine-word carriers with per-consumer offsets reduce slots to 283. This preserves physical producer, phase, port, and exact extraction coordinates, including packed hierarchy rebasing. Both LLVM and Slop consume the same plan.
  • State around compact loops: the old eligibility gate disabled in-place state for an entire graph containing any compact loop. Ordering can be proved for surrounding state while retaining conservative staging inside the loop. The Pyrope RAM had eight staged memories despite the corresponding Verilog memories already being in-place. The register extension around compact loops was rejected after an LRU regression; registers around these loops retain staging. Tests check ordering both within a color and across colors.
  • Redundant buffer clearing: ABI input words are immediately packed, and output words are either explicitly seeded or written by the native kernel. Remove their redundant zero-initialization. Native rolled-loop adapters have the same opportunity; zero-trip outputs are initialized inside the native entry.
  • Dirty tracking: frequent random stimulus makes comparisons, bitmap propagation, and scheduling costly. Disabling it is an explicit diagnostic tuning experiment, not a hidden change to default benchmark settings. RAM Verilog can approach Verilator with this setting. The global default remains appropriate for larger mostly-idle designs.
  • Nested loops and native optimization: Pyrope LRU spends substantial time in the inner LLVM loop and outer C++ loop scheduling. The attempted register extension around loops regressed performance and was reverted. The native pipeline now uses O2 without size attributes, retaining rolled loops while permitting speed-oriented simplification and vectorization. LLVM intrinsics may represent vector reductions, but there are no external body calls inside those native loops.

Paired candidate measurements

Fresh execution and validation

All 390 source LLVM cases were newly executed: 382 passed their recorded full-cycle checksums; 8 retained their expected capability skips; no failures and no cache reuse. Four unit/smoke targets passed, including LLVM and Slop execution of shared-lane and ordered-state fixtures. Corpus check and Python lint passed. Validation record · Latest rows (local artifact) · Outlier binary manifest (local artifact)

The measurements use two immutable compiler snapshots. The later snapshot restores staging for registers around compact loops; all affected root cases were rerun with it. Memory ordering remains enabled around those loops. Unaffected source cases retain the earlier snapshot. Both snapshots contain shared ABI lanes, O2 native kernels, buffer-clearing removal, and the revised reused-module fence policy.

In the serial paired audit, the number of frontend runs at least 2× slower than Verilator fell from 58 to 36; 46 of 92 frontend runs improved by more than 10%. These counts describe the selected outliers, not a corpus geomean. The original report flagged 67 frontend runs; its timings differ from the freshly paired baseline.

Fresh LLVM / Slop comparison

Both backends used identical common color plans. Pyrope RAM additionally emits one LLVM-only standalone loop plan; its shared root plans are byte-identical. All checksums passed. Higher speedup means LLVM is faster. Raw comparison and build times.

Remaining performance work

The work does not make LLVM universally faster than Slop or Verilator. Native kernels use LLVM arithmetic and direct ordered memory accesses, but the C++ scheduler and boundary adapters remain. Nested compact loops also retain outer C++ scheduling. These costs dominate many remaining profiles.

Pyrope br_flow_xbar_rr regressed about 15% in a full-length paired comparison. Its root changed from 19 to 13 colors and peak live words increased from 14 to 27; samples remain concentrated in the root and nested-loop schedulers. Restoring more fences reduces the gap, but does not eliminate it. This is an open regression in the static partition heuristic, not a checksum failure. Full-length regression samples (local artifact) · Explicit fence=0 diagnostic (local artifact).

The scalar-argument cap was rejected: limiting calls to five scalar inputs made Verilog weighted round-robin about 7% slower, as packed-output traffic offset fewer stack arguments. The final source does not contain that cap. Rejected ABI experiment (local artifact).

A follow-up counted rolled-loop body operations times iterations in the fence estimate. It produced byte-identical plans and native objects for Pyrope crossbar, LRU, and RAM, so it did not explain or repair this regression and was removed. Rejected loop-cost experiment (local artifact).

Lower-priority compile investigation: br_delay_valid_next

Source setup was measured separately from host compilation and simulation, with the same frozen compiler, fresh work directories, incremental reuse disabled, and one LLVM worker. Local scratch setup completes in tens of milliseconds; individual native object generation accounts for roughly 12–106 ms across these measurements. The multi-second setup observations in the full benchmark did not reproduce as LLVM optimization cost in isolation. Filesystem effects are material. This source experiment does not claim to resolve the older netlist compile-time outlier; the prior source investigation retains that analysis. Timing records (local artifact).
